After the `龙爪` close at `2026-05-22 18:17:47.929280`, there were `0` new paper trades. Since the prior node start at `20:37:01`, the probe scored `2899` signal decisions into `140` per-token candidates: `fast_profit=10`, `fast_profit_then_collapse=16`, `slow_runner=3`, `flat_timeout=81`, `stop_first=29`, and `missing_path=1`, with policy counts `quick_take_profit=26`, `conditional_slow_hold=3`, and `skip=111`. The best eligible support rule was only `10/22` positive (`45.45%` precision), below the already rejected wide-window `v95_like_pred_rescue` precision of `9/14` (`64.29%`). The current-node slice after `23:14:57` had only `2` time-to-barrier candidates, `0` low-volume breakout candidates, and `0` eligible support rules, so those empty local reports are kept under `.ccg` only. Decision: no new scoreboard row because this is thinner evidence for an already rejected direction; no model artifact change, threshold/sizing/env change, runtime change, live switch, or restart.
- 2026-05-22 post-BUILDGUY read-only diagnostic: research summary `docs/research/20260522-post-buildguy-read-only-diagnostic/summary.md`; read-only reports `data/replay_reports/time_to_barrier_probe_20260522_post_buildguy.json`, `data/replay_reports/support_action_policy_20260522_post_buildguy.json`, and `data/replay_reports/low_volume_breakout_probe_20260522_post_buildguy.json`; external Claude sessions `917aef53-dad0-4f4e-aa20-7dd662d5828a` and `d57b1705-142f-4474-8f79-3951c948f3f9`. Since the BUILDGUY nonce failure at `2026-05-22 17:46:18`, the live canary produced `952` signal decisions with `950` rejects and `2` queued signals: BUILDGUY failed with stale nonce, while `龙爪` passed the current primary gates (`prob=0.9924232830169161`, `PredReturn=59.65499868749451`, `volume_30s=3.172212385073328`, `price_volatility=0.2425841856252674`) and closed by `TRAILING_STOP` after `33.49s` for `+0.0000303781` BNB. `龙爪` reached `+46.33%` MFE versus signal and `+41.42%` versus entry, confirming the current v95 canary can catch a clean short runner; it does not justify loosening gates. The refreshed time-to-barrier probe over `40` rejected candidates found `4` fast-profit, `2` fast-profit-then-collapse, `1` slow-runner, `11` stop-first, and `22` flat-timeout cases; `support_action_policy` still found `0` eligible rules at `min_selected=5`. The low-volume probe over `8` candidates split into `3` fast-profit-then-stop, `4` fakeout, and `1` flat case, so low-volume/short-spike evidence remains mixed. Decision: no model artifact change, no threshold or sizing change, no runtime change, no restart, and no scoreboard model-direction change beyond recording this diagnostic.
- 2026-05-22 BUILDGUY queued buy execution failure: research summary `docs/research/20260522-buildguy-buy-execution-failed/summary.md`; external Claude session `126dcebd-6c94-4e43-996f-00b38a1d122f`. After the prior `17:40:54.307910 +0800` abstention closeout, the live v95 canary produced one queued signal: `BUILDGUY` (`0x18C412942775bff9523B29752A08444986654444`) at `17:46:18.174736`, with `prob=0.9829137733278135`, `PredReturn=47.50809044519591`, `volume_30s=1.6957425722970296`, `price_volatility=0.146165426354468`, and `age=145s`. The bot used fresh lifecycle fast status (`stale=0.018s`, `chain_lag=2.176s`) and attempted a real buy for about `0.000294 BNB`, but `src.core.trader` rejected it at `17:46:19.143` with `nonce too low: next nonce 869, tx nonce 867`. No BUILDGUY row was added to `data/paper_trades.jsonl`, `data/bot_state.json` still had `positions={}`, and no later BUILDGUY `BUY SIGNAL` or open-position audit appeared. Lifecycle attribution showed the signal was already `+55.93%` from first observed price, peaked only another `+4.44%` above signal by `17:46:20`, and was `-27.32%` versus signal by the `17:55:56` runtime snapshot; the model re-evaluation after the failed buy quickly deteriorated to `PredReturn=2.7276` at `17:46:22` and then negative. Decision: classify as a transient execution-layer nonce-staleness failure, not a successful trade and not a model threshold miss. No model artifact, `.env`, threshold, sizing, trading-logic, or restart change; continue watching nonce failures as an execution reliability risk.
- 2026-05-22 post-17:05 abstention plus cleanup audit: research summary `docs/research/20260522-live-no-trade-abstention/summary.md`. Closeout snapshot through `17:40:54.307910 +0800`: since `16:48`, the live v95 canary produced `791` signal decisions, all rejected, with `0` queued buys, `0` opened buys, and `0` new paper-trade rows; since `17:05`, there were `258` additional rejected decisions and still no buys. The later candidates `🤣`, `以太坊思维`, `TER`, `108`, `币安黑奴`, `EHD1000`, `请自行做好研究`, `NNB`, and `田螺姑娘` all stayed below the signal-time PredReturn level needed to justify changing thresholds. The reported "new trade" was the earlier `吃饱饱赚饱饱` `TIME_EXIT` cleanup at `13:52:09`: the bot attempted to sell the already-open `13:42:48` position, found on-chain token balance `0`, and removed it from state; this was an operational zero-balance cleanup, not a new model entry. Because that bot process started at `13:28:59`, before `6142cee` (`fix: persist zero-balance sell cleanup`) was available to the running process, `paper_trades.jsonl` still has the `OPEN` row without a matching synthetic `CLOSE`; current source already records future zero-balance removals in `signal_audit`, and no retroactive synthetic close is added. `AIBNB` remains the main replay watchpoint from the broader slice: it reached about `+65.57%` from first-signal price and then fell about `-51.80%` from peak, so it stays a local borderline case rather than a public model change. Decision: no model artifact change, no threshold/sizing change, no runtime change, no restart, and no scoreboard model-direction update beyond this abstention/cleanup note.
- 2026-05-22 live watchpoint after `16:36 +0800`: research summary `docs/research/20260522-live-watchpoint-wenzhou/summary.md`. The live v95 canary produced `166` signal decisions through `16:48`, with `0` queued buys, `0` opened buys, and `0` new paper-trade rows. `温州队长` was the useful watchpoint: its first signal had `prob=0.9848170688295238`, `PredReturn=4.558862752960975`, and failed on `entry_volume_30s_below_min`; its max-prob signal had `prob=0.9902015387357727`, `PredReturn=-6.624934944561912`, and failed on `pred_return_below_min`. The path did peak `+115.54%` after first signal, but by the closeout snapshot it was about `-62.38%` from peak. Decision: no live action, no model artifact change, no `.env` change, no threshold/sizing change, and no bot restart. Keep this as a replay watchpoint for volume-gated missed-runner analysis, not as evidence to lower volume or PredReturn gates.
- 2026-05-19 flow activation/fakeout probe: live-first evidence compared the clean profitable `赵长娥` trade against recent TSG and x402 failures. SmartSearch research is in `docs/research/20260519-flow-activation-fakeout/summary.md`; the read-only implementation is `src/pipeline/flow_activation_probe.py` plus `scripts/probe_flow_activation.py`; report is `data/replay_reports/flow_activation_probe_20260519_v97.json`. The report scored `521` per-token candidates from `13,423` signal events and accepted only one retrospective candidate: `赵长娥`, classified as `flow_activation_clean_profit` with volume ramp ratio `2.91`, pre-buy pressure `1.0`, and first `+25%` in about `6.13s`. `币安 x402` was classified as `dead_flow_rescue`, and TSG was skipped because PredReturn trajectory did not pass the ramp gate and the path hit stop-loss first. Decision: promote the flow-trajectory and dead-flow-rescue parts to a replay-integrated candidate gate or conditional-exit experiment, but do not treat sell-pressure fakeout as proven yet because the report has `sell_pressure_fakeout=0`. Do not switch live from this probe alone. The report is explicitly `live_switch_evidence=false`, `safe_for_live_gate=false`.
- 2026-05-19 late-pump exhaustion veto replay: live TSG and x402 both supported a path-state exhaustion hypothesis. TSG was an old late chase (`age≈210s`, `PredReturn=39.56`, STOP_LOSS), while x402 was a young peak-then-fade entry that peaked about `+158%` before signal and closed by TIME_EXIT. Research is in `docs/research/20260519-late-pump-exhaustion-veto/summary.md`; implementation is replay-only in `src/pipeline/train_hybrid.py` plus `scripts/run_late_pump_exhaustion_replay.py`; report is `data/replay_reports/late_pump_exhaustion_replay_20260519_v95.json`. The bounded 16-candidate grid used 10% sizing and selected candidate `0`, but validation had `late_pump_veto_reject_count=0` and no improvement over baseline; final confirmation also failed profit/stress gates. Decision: reject and do not switch live. Keep the evidence for richer candidate-level path-state labels or conditional exits, not another broad veto or threshold relaxation.
- 2026-05-19 low-volume breakout/fakeout probe: SmartSearch-backed breakout/fakeout and meta-labeling research led to a read-only probe for v95 `entry_volume_30s_below_min` rejects, saved as `data/replay_reports/low_volume_breakout_probe_20260519_v95.json` with implementation in `src/pipeline/low_volume_breakout_probe.py` and CLI `scripts/probe_low_volume_breakout.py`. Since the v95 restart at `2026-05-19 04:02:23`, the report saw `2680` rejected signal decisions, filtered `26` low-volume decisions, and deduplicated to `16` per-token candidates. Classes were `low_volume_runner=2`, `low_volume_fast_profit_then_stop=5`, `low_volume_fakeout=7`, and `low_volume_flat=2`; policies were `conditional_rescue_probe=2`, `quick_take_profit_probe=5`, and `skip=9`. This confirms the low-volume blocked pocket is mixed: `微信时刻` and `HERMANO` were clean low-volume runners, while `MATRIX-3`, `PI-402`, `币安社区`, `尼罗基金会`, `小鸟咪`, `4lpha`, and `Agora-1` were stop-first fakeouts. Decision: promote this direction to a replay-integrated conditional low-volume rescue plus quick-profit/conditional-hold experiment, but do not switch live and do not lower `MIN_ENTRY_VOLUME_30S` globally. The report is explicitly `live_switch_evidence=false` and keeps the 10% sizing policy unchanged.
- 2026-05-19 time-to-barrier probe: SmartSearch-backed time-to-profit/competing-risk research led to a read-only first-barrier probe over the v95 live window, saved as `data/replay_reports/time_to_barrier_probe_20260519_v95.json` with implementation in `src/pipeline/time_to_barrier_probe.py` and CLI `scripts/probe_time_to_barrier.py`. Since the v95 restart at `2026-05-19 04:02:23`, the probe saw `2042` rejected signal decisions, deduplicated to `66` token candidates, and recorded run-time SHA-256 fingerprints for the live input files. Each input is read once into a bytes snapshot, so hashes and parsing use those same bytes; current collector/bot input paths may change after the report is written. Classes were `fast_profit=12`, `fast_profit_then_collapse=8`, `slow_runner=1`, `stop_first=7`, and `flat_timeout=38`; policies were `quick_take_profit=20`, `conditional_slow_hold=1`, and `skip=45`. Under a stricter sanity filter of `prob>=0.94` and `PredReturn>=0`, `11/19` candidates were quick-take-profit, `1/19` was slow-runner, and `7/19` were skip. Decision: promote this direction to a replay-integrated conditional entry/exit experiment, but do not switch live. The report is explicitly `live_switch_evidence=false`; the next test must keep 10% sizing, preserve v95/v84 as the primary candidate generator, and reject the direction if validation/final/walk-forward/stress fail versus the current best v95 baseline.
- 2026-05-19 rare-runner candidate ranker probe: SmartSearch-backed ranking research led to a read-only `CatBoostRanker` / `YetiRank` probe over the existing v95 candidate set, saved as `data/replay_reports/v96_candidate_ranker_probe_recent4_20260519.json` with supporting implementation in `src/pipeline/candidate_ranker_probe.py`. The full-file attempts were stopped because rebuilding all lifecycle samples on the live machine used more than 5GB RAM for several minutes, so the falsification used four explicit closed lifecycle files; the report records the exact lifecycle file paths and SHA-256 fingerprints. Validation improved over the existing entry-value ordering (`45.0` vs `41.0` top relevance, clean runners `12` vs `9`, collapses `1` vs `1`), but final was worse (`60.0` vs `61.0`, clean runners `15` vs `15`, collapses `5` vs `4`). Do not switch live or integrate this ranker into replay/live. Treat the result as evidence that a generic YetiRank layer on the current entry features is too weak; the next model direction should use live path state more directly, such as conditional exit/re-entry logic around STOP_LOSS or PPO runner-retention events.
- 2026-05-19 stop-loss re-entry / runner-retention probe: a new read-only probe over `paper_trades`, `signal_audit`, collector runtime state, and latest lifecycle incrementals found a narrow conditional-exit opportunity, not a live-switch candidate. Latest run `data/replay_reports/reentry_retention_probe_20260519_050349.json` classified `6` STOP_LOSS re-entry candidates with `1` accepted (`币安小子`: `+25` in about `18.1s`, `+60` in about `53.1s`, MFE about `+141.2%`) and `6` PPO runner-retention candidates with `1` accepted (`何赵`: `+25` in about `31.0s`, MFE about `+34.8%`). It correctly rejected `WAGMI` and `B402`; `BISMILLAH` reclaimed quickly but hit `-25` inside the post-reclaim guard window, so it is treated as fake-runner risk. This supports a replay-level conditional re-entry / runner-retention experiment, not global threshold lowering, blanket longer hold, or blanket partial exits.
- 2026-05-19 replay/live alignment fix: `model_replay.live_replay_config_from_manifest` and `train_hybrid._run_eval_replay` now preserve the manifest near-threshold rescue gate (`buy_near_*`) through runtime, all-in, stress, and walk-forward replay. The corrected v95 final replay matches the accepted v95 manifest exactly on headline metrics (`511.4778%` return, `0.03067113` BNB profit, `44` trades), with `near_threshold_entry_count=3`. Treat any earlier replay report missing `buy_near_threshold_min_prob` as a v84-like replay, not a valid v95 replay.
- 2026-05-19 selective near-threshold probe accepted v95 as a canary candidate. Live evidence came first: no new real trade after WAGMI, but high-score rejects showed `ERNIE` as a clean missed runner (`prob=0.9490`, `PredReturn=35.1430`, `volume_30s=1.2743`, MFE `+53.189%`) while two `BNBROCK` near-misses were correct skips. The implemented rule is deliberately narrow: keep v84 primary threshold at `0.98`, add only a `0.94-0.98` rescue band with `entry_score>=32`, `volume_30s>=1.25`, `price_volatility>=0.08`, and the existing max-age bound. This is structurally different from the rejected global `0.95` threshold sweep because the primary threshold remains unchanged and the rescue gate is manifest-scoped to v95. Risk note: the validation sweep did not discriminate strongly (`144/144` rules passed; top rule had only `2` near episodes and `0` trade delta), and the final improvement is one extra trade, so live canary attribution is mandatory before promoting v95 to durable baseline.
- 2026-05-19 01:40 live-first cycle: bot and collector were healthy under `memectl`/tmux, `.env` still used live v67 with 10% sizing, state had `0` open positions and `0.005079303120051795` BNB balance. There were no real trades after the WAGMI close. Since WAGMI close there were `2493` rejected signal decisions: `2470` `pred_return_below_min` and `23` `buy_model_reject`. The newest high-PredReturn near miss, `FELON`, peaked at `PredReturn=42.0238`, `prob=0.9536`, and `volume_30s=1.4657`, then only reached about `+6.99%` MFE and hit the `-18%` stop zone about `68s` after the signal. `Scamlon`, `RVM`, and `ELONG` also showed no runner path. This confirms the recent live issue is still narrow separation: most high-looking rejects are correct skips, while `xPBNB` remains the clean missed-runner outlier. The next experiment should be the already researched v84-like candidate-level runner/collapse gate, not global threshold lowering, volume relaxation, blanket partial exits, or broader profit-path training.
- v75b used `profit_path` behavior cloning, `min_policy_hold_seconds=60`, `trailing_start_pct=0.35`, `trailing_stop_pct=0.12`, `stop_loss=-0.25`, `entry_delay_seconds=1`, `exit_delay_seconds=4`, and 10% position sizing.
- v75b is useful evidence that simply forcing longer minimum hold is not enough. The next promising direction is conditional exit logic: keep runners longer only when path/flow features justify it, while preserving fast exits for collapsing tokens.
- v76 is useful evidence that making the buy label stricter is not enough. Recent near-miss analysis found a small runner subset among high `PredReturn` rejects, but most high `PredReturn` rejects still had large downside. The next direction should be a second-stage runner filter or conditional exit policy, not a global threshold relaxation.
- v77 confirms the same shape with a softer threshold: some runner-like cases exist in high `PredReturn` rejects, but a single global quality threshold still leaves too much money on the table versus v67. The next step should be a dedicated runner classifier or conditional exit path, not another threshold sweep.
- v78 confirms that using average-path return as the primary buy label is too restrictive. It raises apparent win rate but collapses trade count and turns both walk-forward and stress replay negative, so the next candidate should separate runner discovery from dump avoidance instead of replacing the main buy label with an average-path target.
- The latest-calibrated v67 `min_entry_score=35/40` sweep confirms entry-value gating is useful as a risk filter but not sufficient as a standalone improvement. It improves validation and harsh-stress robustness, but final return stays below the accepted hold75 baseline.
- A quick validation falsification of lower thresholds showed that `buy_threshold=0.95/0.94/0.93` plus simple young-token, volume, and volatility filters admits too many weak signals. The `0.95` and `0.94` variants lost money with extreme drawdown, while `0.93` needed 1281 validation trades and still had `-64.4315%` max drawdown. Do not lower the global threshold without a learned second-stage gate.
- A 2026-05-17 post-restart near-miss check again rejected global threshold lowering: seven `prob>=0.93` rejects with available 300s paths mostly had only `0-8%` max upside before falling `-25%` to `-59%`; the best short spike (`CC`) reached about `+34%` but later faded to about `-22%`. This points to conditional runner/exit modeling, not broader entry.
- Early trailing was falsified as a simple fix for short spikes. On the current latest-calibrated validation split, v67 hold75 baseline returned `195.7659%`; `trailing=30%/10%` returned `183.0245%`, and `trailing=25%/10%` returned `178.6817%`. Do not switch to globally earlier trailing.
- Research note: Hudson & Thames' meta-labeling writeup frames the method as a secondary model that learns when to use a primary model and describes triple-barrier labels as path-based labels rather than simple next-step direction labels. For this repo, the next viable experiment should preserve v67 as the primary model and train a second-stage take/skip or runner classifier on v67 candidate signals, using path/barrier outcomes and live execution costs. Source: https://hudsonthames.org/does-meta-labeling-add-to-signal-efficacy-triple-barrier-method/
- Future research notes must be reproducible. When a rejected or accepted model is influenced by outside research, create `docs/research/<YYYYMMDD>-<slug>/`, run `smart-search deep` first to generate `plan.json`, execute the planned SmartSearch discovery/fetch commands, and record fetched source links plus the key commands that influenced the experiment.
- In this scoreboard, "search", "research", "look up", "查找资料", "网上调查", and "深度搜索" mean SmartSearch Deep Research: `smart-search deep` plan first, then planned `smart-search search`/`exa-search`/`zhipu-search`/`context7-*`/`map`/`fetch` evidence. A standalone broad search result is discovery only, not proof.
- Do not use native Codex web browsing or uncited model memory as evidence for model-method decisions. If SmartSearch is unavailable, record the blocker; do not silently downgrade to ordinary search.
- Do not cite broad search summaries as proof in this scoreboard. If a claim changes model structure, labels, features, exits, or live deployment logic, it needs a fetched source saved under `docs/research/` and a `summary.md` explaining how the evidence changed the experiment.
- v67 hold60 is accepted as an exit-parameter upgrade, not a new buy model. A same-data check versus hold40 showed `267.5674%` vs `259.4872%` final return, `64.5455%` vs `59.0909%` win rate, `-21.0330%` vs `-23.7954%` max drawdown, `41.1429%` vs `30.1828%` walk-forward worst return, and stronger harsh stress replay. This matches live evidence that some profitable runners were being cut too quickly, while keeping the hard stop, trailing, and 10% sizing unchanged.
- v79 shows that a simple `live_target_hit_before_stop` barrier label with a `25%` target is too permissive for this data. It generated many low-quality entries and failed even after raising the threshold, so the next profitable direction should preserve v67's high-quality entry model and focus on conditional runner exits or second-stage filtering rather than replacing the buy target wholesale.
- v67 hold75 is accepted as a latest-calibration exit-parameter upgrade, not a new buy model. Under 2026-05-17 execution calibration (`entry_delay=1`, `entry_max_fill_wait=4`, `entry_price_protection=7.9784%`, `exit_delay=7`, `exit_max_fill_wait=8`) and explicit `max_hold_seconds=300`, validation improved from hold60 `182.7473%` to `195.7659%` return, `61.0000%` to `63.0000%` win rate, `44.0271%` to `48.8485%` walk-forward worst return, and harsh execution from `-0.5943%` to `15.7276%`. Final return improved from `187.7406%` to `194.7110%`; final drawdown and walk-forward were slightly worse but remained within gate, with harsh stress still positive.
- v80 shows that token-balanced sample weighting is not a standalone meta-labeling fix. It can surface many more trades and inflate headline return, but without a strict second-stage gate it overfits active token clusters and destroys final walk-forward robustness. Any future token-balanced experiment must constrain the primary threshold/trade count or use it only inside a true v67-candidate take/skip gate.
- A 2026-05-18 fast falsification of `buy_sample_weighting=recency_decay` on the target-hit60 / entry-value stack did not beat v84. With `max_samples_per_token=20`, `buy_recency_half_life_hours=36`, and `catboost_iterations=100`, the holdout run reached `65.8447%` final return, `-10.3151%` max drawdown, `4.2660%` WF worst return, `22` trades, `5.6425%` harsh friction, and `-6.9837%` harsh execution. The implementation is useful, but the current weighting curve is not a baseline improvement.
- v67 `entry_value` / `min_entry_score=40` is accepted as a runtime gate upgrade, not a new buy model. It uses the already shipped diagnostic entry-value model as the live `PredReturn` filter. On the 2026-05-18 current-data full validation it improved return from `193.4369%` to `201.5949%`, win rate from `63.0000%` to `66.2162%`, and WF worst return from `47.8686%` to `64.1369%`. On final it improved return from `187.9662%` to `189.6565%`, win rate from `61.9565%` to `67.6923%`, max drawdown from `-21.5063%` to `-15.6950%`, and harsh execution from `13.6437%` to `35.1696%`. The earlier 2026-05-17 `min_entry_score=40` rejection was under the older same-data hold75 check without this final current-data acceptance pass.
- A follow-up entry-protection sweep on the same v67 runtime gate showed that widening `entry_price_protection_pct` materially improves live-sized replay and stress. The best nearby offline compromise was `entry_price_protection_pct=0.18` with `min_entry_score=45`, which reached `220.2125%` final return, `-16.7280%` max drawdown, `40.3773%` WF worst return, and positive harsh stress (`74.6735%` harsh execution, `63.4903%` harsh friction). It is better than the accepted `min_entry_score=40` runtime gate on return, walk-forward, and stress, but the live bot was left unchanged per user instruction and should be restarted into this candidate later.
- The latest v84 hold-window sweep promotes `max_hold_seconds=560`, not `420`, `480`, `540`, `580`, or `600`. The 560s point has the best final return/profit plus stronger harsh friction and harsh execution than 540s, while 580/600 both lose stress robustness.
- The v84 `min_policy_hold_seconds=60` transfer test was rejected. The v86b-specific hold60 improvement does not generalize to the stronger v84 model; v84 stays at `min_policy_hold_seconds=45`.
- The v86b delay-robust probe confirms that path-aware labels and profit-path behavior cloning can improve over the live v67 baseline, but not enough to beat v84. Within v86b, the profitable adjustment was increasing `min_policy_hold_seconds` from `45` to `60` while keeping `min_entry_score=35`; raising `min_entry_score` improved risk but cut too much return, and shortening the hold floor to `20` degraded both return and drawdown.
- A 2026-05-18 live near-miss attribution pass falsified simple entry-volume relaxation as the next v84 improvement. In the latest four-hour signal audit window, high `PredReturn` rejects mostly weakened or hit stop-loss conditions. A local live-delay path simulation of `pred_return>=40` candidates showed the current live-style gate (`volume_30s>=1.5`) would have selected 9 simulated entries with only `22.2%` win rate and about `-66%` summed gross return across candidates; relaxing to `volume_30s>=1.25` or `>=1.0` did not create a runner set and increased collapse exposure. Keep v84's `min_entry_volume_30s=1.5` until a learned second-stage runner/collapse classifier proves otherwise.
- v87b falsified the idea of using strict delay-robust return directly as the `entry_value` live gate. The final split had signals but no trades because every candidate failed `min_entry_score=35`; this protects capital but cannot improve live profitability. Keep delay-robust path data as a feature/label component, but the next model should learn a softer take/skip meta-label on plausible primary candidates.
- v88 shows that simply softening the delay-robust regression target to average delayed return is still not enough. It can be made to trade at lower thresholds, but the resulting basket has much lower win rate, much worse drawdown, and far weaker walk-forward return than v84. The evidence points to a targeted runner/collapse meta-label, not another broad threshold or volume relaxation.
- v89 shows that pushing the target-hit barrier up to 80% still does not beat v84. It improves over v88 on validation and final return, but the final set remains below v84 and stress collapses hard. The next step should be a dedicated runner/collapse gate or a conditional exit model, not a stricter primary target.
- v91 shows that a raw binary runner-probability `entry_value` gate is not enough. At live-useful thresholds it is too sparse, while lower thresholds explode into 1400+ final trades with `-56%` to `-61%` drawdown and near-`-100%` walk-forward worst return. The live failure pattern is still early fake runners, but the next model needs candidate-level meta-labeling or conditional exit structure rather than replacing the regression gate with a raw binary hit-before-stop score.
- v93 shows that token-balanced sampling is still not enough even when the runtime threshold and entry-value gate are constrained back toward the v84 stack. It recovers trade count and headline return versus v91, but stress robustness and validation walk-forward remain weak, so this line should not replace v84. The next iteration should focus on conditional exits or a stricter candidate-level meta-label built around v84-style primary candidates.
- The partial-exit runtime sweep on v84 confirms the same direction: the existing PPO policy can harvest more runner value when `allow_partial_exits=True`, but the validation replay weakens enough that it should not replace the baseline as-is. Treat it as evidence that a trained conditional-exit model is worth building, not as a blanket live toggle.
- The 2026-05-19 v84 near-threshold sweep confirms that missed runners are real but not separable by a global threshold relaxation. Lowering `buy_threshold` to `0.95` captured enough final runners to lift final return to `498.6497%`, but validation remained weak at `134.5177%` return and `-31.7694%` walk-forward worst drawdown. This should feed a second-stage runner/collapse classifier, not a live threshold cut.
- 2026-05-18 live attribution adds two separate failure shapes. The current live v67 stack bought `WAGMI` (`0x91d1AAcA03B789D2Df93A0857655aDE813A44444`) at `21:41:57`, with `prob=0.9739`, `PredReturn=52.89`, favorable entry slippage, and fast lifecycle status. It still closed by `STOP_LOSS` after `21.29s` for `-0.00018272` BNB. The lifecycle path shows post-entry MFE of about `+18.20%` by `21:42:02`, then MAE of about `-30.65%` by receipt exit. This is not a speed failure; it is evidence for a conditional early-profit/runner-exit policy that can harvest brief rebounds before the stop cascade.
- The same 2026-05-18 high-PredReturn rejection sweep found one major missed runner, `王福满` (`0x78df06C3C48c63E1FA51e9223EFC004f8E844444`): it was rejected around `21:36:35-21:36:40` because `PredReturn`/buy probability were just under the live gates, yet the 300s path reached roughly `+346%` to `+476%` MFE and stayed strongly positive. However, among `21` rejected signals with `PredReturn>=35` and available paths, `19` ended below the signal path base and `13` had MAE worse than `-25%`. Do not lower the global threshold; use this as evidence for a narrow second-stage "missed runner" detector with strict risk gates.
- v94 confirms that training a broad profit-path partial-exit policy is still too permissive. The live-first pass before the run found WAGMI was already below the -18% stop zone about 4 seconds after signal, while most high-PredReturn rejects after WAGMI also hit -18% or worse; xPBNB remained the rare clean missed runner. v94 did not learn that narrow separation: calibration selected a fallback `0.941` threshold, final win rate fell to `26.2799%`, validation walk-forward worst return fell to `-68.9524%`, and stress replay was strongly negative. Next work should avoid blanket partial-exit/profit-path training and instead build a candidate-level runner/collapse filter anchored to v84-like primary candidates plus live path features.
- 2026-05-28 train-boundary preserve-base follow-up: rejected by strict gate, but it is the first train-boundary soft-feature follow-up that improved both validation and final net profit. Report `data/replay_reports/runner_retention_candidate_gate_replay_20260528_train_boundary_soft_feature_preserve_base_score060_grid.json` reran the score060 sampled soft feature with `--preserve-base-candidates`. Validation net profit improved `0.019254464794 -> 0.020696672022` BNB and final net profit improved `0.006994210572 -> 0.007545463282` BNB; validation/final walk-forward worst return and stress worst net profit also improved. It still failed acceptance because win rate fell (`0.84375 -> 0.775` validation, `0.66667 -> 0.61538` final) and drawdown worsened (`-8.1825% -> -17.8021%` validation, `-12.9081% -> -14.7639%` final, plus weaker stress drawdown). Decision: no live switch, no `.env`, model artifact, threshold, sizing, or bot restart change. Preserve-base is the best current direction from this branch; next experiment should keep base entries preserved and raise the rescue/path score floor, starting with `buy_path_state_meta_gate_min_score=0.75`.
- 2026-05-28 train-boundary preserve-base score075 follow-up: rejected, and equivalent to score060. Report `data/replay_reports/runner_retention_candidate_gate_replay_20260528_train_boundary_soft_feature_preserve_base_score075_grid.json` raised `buy_path_state_meta_gate_min_score` to `0.75` under `--preserve-base-candidates`, but validation/final trade counts and all headline metrics matched the score060 preserve-base run: validation net profit `0.019254464794 -> 0.020696672022` BNB, final net profit `0.006994210572 -> 0.007545463282` BNB, with the same win-rate and drawdown failures. Decision: no live switch, no `.env`, model artifact, threshold, sizing, or bot restart change. This falsifies score-floor tightening in the `0.60 -> 0.75` band; next work should constrain a different rescue axis such as `buy_near_min_pred_return`, `buy_near_min_entry_volume_30s`, or a direct rescue-probability/rank cap while preserving base entries.
- 2026-05-28 train-boundary preserve-base trade-delta attribution: diagnostic only, no live switch. Report `data/replay_reports/runner_retention_candidate_gate_replay_20260528_train_boundary_soft_feature_preserve_base_score075_trade_delta.json` shows why preserve-base raises net profit but fails risk gates. Validation added trades were `8/13` winners with `+736.1336%` return sum while removed baseline trades were `4/5` winners with `+470.5714%`; final added trades were only `1/7` winners with `+57.1739%`, while removed baseline trades were mostly losers (`1/5` winners, `-47.4278%`). This means the net-profit gain comes from replacing final baseline losers and one large final added winner, but the added rescue set is too loss-heavy for win-rate and drawdown gates. Feature contrast points to hot-extension risk, not low-volume risk: final added stop-loss rows had higher `early_buy_volume`, `volume_30s`, `price_volatility`, `total_buy_volume`, and `trade_frequency`. Next work should add parameterized rescue-side ceiling filters for hot-extension features and test them under `--preserve-base-candidates`, rather than raising minimum volume or rerunning score-floor sweeps.
